###### STORYLINES: VIRGINIA VS. DELAWARE

###### INSIDE SATURDAY'S MATCHUP

## TBA INSIDE SATURDAY'S MATCHUP •

• Saturday is the final non-conference game on UVA’s 2026 regular-season schedule. Its final eight games are against ACC opponents.
• Virginia and Delaware will meet for the first time ever on Saturday. The Fightin' Blue Hens are the second of two first-time opponents in 
2026 (Norfolk State).

• After Saturday, Virginia will have played schools from all but 14 of the 50 United States in football. UVA has yet to play a school from 
Alaska, Arizona, Arkansas, Hawaii, Iowa, Maine, Montana, Nebraska, Nevada, New Mexico, North Dakota, Rhode Island, South Dakota and 
Vermont.

• Saturday's contest marks Virginia’s sixth against an active member of Conference USA. The Blue Hens joined the league in 2025. UVA is 
2-3 all-time against Conference USA opponents, with its last game coming against Old Dominion in 2019, a 28-17 Cavalier victory at 
Scott Stadium.

• Virginia and Delaware are among the 27 FBS teams that have yet to lose a fumble in 2026. UVA is one of 15 teams nationally that have 
no more than one turnover through three games played.

• The Virginia defense has forced a turnover in 12 of its last 14 games dating back to last season. The Cavaliers have forced a total of 23 
turnovers (17 INT, 6 FR) in that span.

• Sixteen Cavaliers have caught a pass this season, second only to Miami (18)  for the most in the ACC. In its last two games, UVA has had 
at least 10 pass catchers.

• The Cavaliers enter Saturday’s contest ranked 11th in the country in pass efficiency (187.95). Quarterback **Beau Pribula** has completed 
21 of his first 25 passes of the 2026 season.

###### TOP STORYLINES

• The Virginia football program will honor Lavel Davis Jr., Devin Chandler and D’Sean Perry as part of UVA Strong Day at Scott Stadium. The 
three football players tragically lost their lives in an on-campus shooting on Nov. 13, 2022. Permanent memorials are displayed on the 
south end zone fascia at Scott Stadium, with additional plaques located along UVA’s “Legends Walk” on the north end zone concourse.

• Saturday’s matchup pits Delaware’s passing offense, ranked No. 11 nationally in passing yards per game (325.7 ypg), against Virginia’s 
passing defense, which has allowed an average of 150.0 passing through its first three games, the second fewest in the ACC and 19th 
in FBS. UVA has not allowed 300 yards passing in 11 straight games (Oct. 4, 2025) nor has it allowed 200 yards passing since Nov. 15, 
2025, a span of six games.

• Virginia quarterback **Beau Pribula’s** brother, Cade, played three seasons at Delaware from 2019-21 before finishing his career at Sacred 
Heart. Delaware wide receiver Nick Tyree’s brother, Chris, appeared in 10 games for Virginia during the 2024 season as a wide receiver 
and kick returner.

• Current UVA running back **Jekail Middlebrook** accounted for 147 all-purpose yards (68 rush, 53 receiving, 26 punt return) and a rushing 
touchdown against Delaware while playing for Middle Tennessee State last season. The Blue Hens hung on for a 31-28 victory in Newark.

• Current Delaware recruiting coordinator/running backs coach Xavier Dye was a graduate assistant coach at Clemson for two seasons  
(2017-18) under then-Tiger offensive coordinator Tony Elliott. The two overlapped for a pair of ACC Championships, two College Football 
Playoff appearances and the 2018 national championship.

Fralin Family Head Football Coach **Tony Elliott** will take part in a postgame press conference in the Alumni 
Room at Bryant Hall. Following Elliott's press conference, select players will be brought to the Alumni Room 
to meet with media representatives. UVA athletic communications personnel will take player requests during 
the fourth quarter of each game. Virginia’s locker room is closed to media following games, both at home 
and on the road.

###### ACC MEDIA PORTAL

The ACC Media Portal has moved to a new service provider. The Conference will continue to provide video 
from a variety of press conferences and select ACC Championship footage throughout the athletic year. 
Game highlights from regular season football and basketball games will no longer be provided. Previous 
accounts to the ACC Media Portal will no longer be active. If you are interested in access to the new ACC 
Media Portal, please contact Slim Vollinger, svollinger@theacc.org to gain access and for additional 
information. The use of ACC Championship highlights provided through the ACC Media Portal remain subject

###### THE UNIVERSITY OF VIRGINIA

###### UNIQUELY UVA

The University of Virginia is an institution rich with history and tradition. Here is some of the 
terminology that contributes to UVA’s culture.

Grounds: The term used by students, faculty and alumni to refer to the University dating as far back as Thomas Jefferson. “Campus” is never used.

First Year, Second Year, Third Year, Fourth Year: Instead of freshman, sophomore, junior, senior. Why you may ask? To be a “senior” implies that a person has reached 
the final phase of learning, a feat that Thomas Jefferson believed to be impossible, arguing instead that education is a life-long process.

Commonwealth: Virginia is one of four constituent states of the United States of America that officially uses the name “Commonwealth” instead of the word state in 
all references.  The other three commonwealths are Kentucky, Massachusetts and Pennsylvania.

###### UNIVERSITY NICKNAMES

Virginia’s athletics teams have been accompanied by a somewhat confusing array of nicknames. The most prominent and widely accepted of these monikers are 
“Cavaliers,” “Wahoos” and “Hoos.”

Although the terms “Cavaliers,” “Wahoos” and “Hoos” are used almost interchangeably to refer to University teams and players, “Cavaliers” is more often used by the 
media, while “Wahoos” and “Hoos” are frequently used by Virginia students and fans.

Legend has it that Washington & Lee baseball fans dubbed the Virginia players “Wahoos” during the fiercely contested rivalry that existed between the two in-state 
schools in the 1890s. By 1940, “Wahoos”

was in general use around Grounds to denote University students or events relating to them. The abbreviated “Hoos” sprang up later in student newspapers and has 
gained growing popularity in recent years.

###### THE CAVALIER MASCOT

The costumed Cavalier with a large character head debuted during Virginia’s 1984 football season and has remained the official mascot of the University.

The first documented Cavalier on horseback was Francis Bell, a Virginia student from Dublin, Va., in 1947. Bell rode onto the field dressed as Cavaliers for Virginia’s 
home football game with Harvard on the afternoon of October 11.

The Cavalier on horseback returned as the University’s mascot in 1963. The UVA Polo Club provided both the horse and rider. However, the mounted Cavalier and his 
horse parted company in 1974 with the inception of AstroTurf at Scott Stadium.

The mounted Cavalier made its return in the Florida Citrus Bowl at the end of the 1989 football season. Due to its instant popularity, the Cavalier on horseback 
returned the following season on a regular basis and continues to lead the Virginia football team onto the field at the beginning of all home games.

Since 2000, Kim Kirschnick led the Cavaliers out of the tunnel on horseback and retired after the 2021 season finale against Virginia Tech. 2022 marked Julie 
Caruccio first season as the mounted Cavalier. A former interim dean of students at UVA, Caruccio is the first female in school history to have the mounted Cavalier 
job on a full-time basis. When the tradition rotated among members of the club polo team until Kirschnick took over in 2000, at least two women – Bess Worrall 
and Kerry McDaniel – were on horseback for a few games in the early ’90s.

###### UNIVERSITY COLORS

Orange and blue were adopted as the University of Virginia’s official athletic colors at a mass student meeting in 1888. UVA athletic teams had previously worn silver gray and cardinal red, but those colors did not stand out on muddy football fields, prompting a student movement to change them.

One of the students attending the mass meeting was Allen Potts, a star athlete who played on Virginia’s first football team in 1888. Potts showed up at the meeting 
wearing a navy blue-and-orange scarf that he had acquired during a summer boating expedition at Oxford University. Orange and blue were chosen as the official 
athletic colors after one of Potts’ fellow students pulled the scarf off Potts’ neck and, waving it to the crowd, yelled, “How will this do?” UVA later served as the inspiration for schools like Auburn and Florida when they selected school colors.

###### WAH-HOO-WA

The origin of “Wah-hoo-wa” is uncertain. The cheer was used to root on Virginia teams as early as 1890 and may have been borrowed from Dartmouth College, 
whose athletics teams were once known as the Indians.

Legend attributes the yell to Natalie Floyd Otey, who sang the ballad “Where’er You Are, There Shall My Love Be” at Charlottesville’s Levy Opera House in 1893. The 
predominantly student audience noticed that Otey warbled the first three words of the song between each of the stanzas and decided to join in the refrain. By evening’s end, goes the legend, the crowd had corrupted “Where’er You Are” into “Wah-hoo-wah.”

The Levy Opera House stands at the corner of High Street and Park Street and has since been renovated into an office building.

**A WIN FOR THE CAVALIERS WOULD...**

• Be their ninth in their last 10 home games since 
the start of the 2025 season.

• Also be their fourth consecutive at Scott Stadium.

• Mark the second straight over a Conference USA 
opponent, having defeated ODU 28-17 on Sept. 
21, 2019.

• Improve their all-time record against CUSA 
opponents to 3-3.

• Mark the third consecutive 3-1 start to the season 
under head coach **Tony Elliott.**

###### A FIRST FOR EVERYTHING

• Virginia has won eight of its last nine matchups 
against first-time opponents, including its last three 
(Norfolk State, Washington State and Stanford).

• Since 2000, the Cavaliers are 18-11 overall in 
inaugural matchups, including 13-7 at Scott 
Stadium.

###### UPCOMING MILESTONES

• Quarterback **Beau Pribula** needs 31 rushing 
yards to reach 1,000, 121 yards passing for the 
3,000th, and 152 yards of total offense for the 
4,000th of his career.

• The next touchdown for running back **Jekail 
Middlebrook** will be the 15th of his career. He 
currently has nine rushing TDs and four receiving 
TDs.

• Receiver **Jacquon Gibson** needs seven receptions 
to reach 100 for his career and three receiving 
yards for the 1,000th of his career.

• Wide receiver **Rico Flores Jr.** needs 121 yards 
receiving for the 1,000th of his career.

• The next appearance for defensive end **Fisher 
Camac** will be the 40th of his career. Camac also 
needs 2.0 sacks for the 15th of his career.

• Linebacker **Kam Robinson** needs 2.0 sacks for the 
10th of his career.

• Defensive lineman **Justin Townsend** needs one 
tackle to reach 100 for his career.

• Defensive end **Nnanna Anyanwu** needs 2.5 sacks 
for the 10th of his career.

• Kicker **Will Bettridge** needs 10 points for the 350th 
of his career.

• Punter **Daniel Sparks,** who leads all active FBS 
players in games played (61), needs 242 yards 
punting to reach 10,000 for his UVA career. Sparks 
also leads all active FBS players in total yards 
punting (11,095) and total punts (248).

###### BETTRIDGE ON UVA’S CAREER SCORING LIST

• In UVA's second game of the season against 
Norfolk State (9/11), placekicker **Will Bettridge**
set the program's all-time scoring record, 
surpassing former UVA kicker Connor Hughes.

• Hughes' previous all-time record of 332 points 
stood for 21 years.

• In 2025, Bettridge averaged 8.6 points per game, 
good for third among all ACC players and 13th 
nationally.

• For the third consecutive season, Bettridge led all 
UVA scorers in 2025 with 121 points (24-30 FG, 
49-49 PAT).

###### YEAR FIVE UNDER TONY ELLIOTT

• **Tony Elliott** is in his fifth year at the helm of the 
Virginia football program.

• In 2025, Elliott steered UVA to the program's 
most successful season ever as the Cavaliers 
won a program-record 11 games, and made their 
second-ever appearance in the ACC Championship 
game.

• The Wahoos also defeated No. 25 Missouri in the  
TaxSlayer Gator Bowl, 13-7, which marked UVA's 
first bowl win in seven years.

• For his efforts, Elliott was named the ACC Coach of 
the Year by his peers and the Associated Press. He 
also was named the Bear Bryant ACC Coach of the 
Year and took home the Bobby Ross Coach of the 
Year Award from the Touchdown Club of Richmond.

• He was also named a finalist for the Eddie 
Robinson, Paul “Bear” Bryant and George Munger 
national Coach of the Year awards.

• Additionally, Elliott returned all but one of his 
assistant coaches from 2025. The only departure 
was offensive line coach Terry Heffernan, who 
accepted the offensive coordinator position at 
Stanford following the 2025 season. Joey Orck, 
who previously held the title of assistant offensive 
line coach, was promoted to offensive line coach.

• Elliott brought a wealth of championship 
experience to Charlottesville following an 11-
year tenure at Clemson University, his alma 
mater. Elliott helped the Tigers win national 
championships in 2016 and 2018 and seven ACC 
championships (2011, 2015, 2016, 2017, 2018, 
2019, 2020).

• In his 16 years as a full-time college coach prior 
to his arrival in Charlottesville, Elliott has been 
a part of 15 winning seasons including 10-win 
seasons at Clemson from 2011-20.

• With Elliott as offensive coordinator, Clemson went 
89-10 and made four CFP National Championship 
game appearances.

###### FOURTH-QUARTER EMPHASIS

• Over the previous two seasons (2024-25), 
Virginia improved in the fourth quarter, a point of 
emphasis by head coach **Tony Elliott.**

• In that same time span, UVA outscored its 
opponents 146-125 in fourth quarters and 170-
144 when factoring in overtime periods.

• The Cavaliers achieved a 3-1 record in overtime 
games last season with wins over then-No. 
8 Florida State (46-38), Louisville (30-27), 
North Carolina (17-16). Their lone defeat in 
overtime came against Duke (27-20) in the ACC 
Championship game at Bank of America Stadium 
in Charlotte, N.C.

• In Elliott’s first two seasons at UVA, the Cavaliers 
were outscored in fourth quarters 43-36 in 2022 
and 114-54 in 2023.

###### WAHOOS’ DEFENSIVE DELIVERANCE

• Until its 38-27 setback against West Virginia 
(Sept. 19), Virginia's defense – led by defensive 
coordinator **John Rudzinski** – had not allowed 
more than two touchdowns from scrimmage in 
regulation over its previous seven games.

• Dating back to their 2025 season opener against 
Coastal Carolina (Aug. 30), the Cavaliers have won 
12 of their last 13 games when forcing at least 
one turnover. UVA has forced a turnover in 13 of 
its last 17 games.

• In its Week 0 season opener, UVA held NC State 
to only eight points after holding the Wolfpack 
scoreless on their first eight drives of the game.

• Virginia's 19.6 points-allowed-per-game average 
was good for second in the ACC in 2025.

• UVA also finished in the top-5 in forced fumbles 
(11; 2nd), sacks (31; 4th) and interceptions (14; 
T-4th).

• The Cavaliers gained momentum as the 2025 
season progressed, closing out the year in strong 
fashion. In UVA's last eight games of 2025, its 
opponents averaged only 13.9 points per game 
(in regulation), a span in which it did not allow 
more than 21 points in any game.

in one-score games over the course of Tony

###### TONY ELLIOTT: ONE OF 14 AFRICAN AMERICAN HEAD COACHES IN FBS

• Fralin Family Head Football Coach **Tony Elliott** is 
one of only 14 African American FBS head football 
coaches.

• The ACC leads all P4 conferences with three 
black head coaches, including Elliott, Fran Brown 
(Syracuse) and James Franklin (Virginia Tech).

• Entering his fifth season, Elliott was named 
Virginia's 41st head coach on Dec. 10, 2021. 
He is also UVA's second African American head 
football coach, following Mike London, who led the 
program from 2010-15.

###### 2025 STORYLINES IN REVIEW

• With a record of 7-1 in conference play, Virginia 
finished alone atop the ACC standings for the first 
time in program history. As a result, Tony Elliott was 
named ACC Coach of the Year.

• The Cavaliers won a school-record 11 wins, 
capped by a 13-7 triumph over No. 25 Missouri in 
the TaxSlayer Gator Bowl.

• The win in the Gator Bowl was UVA's first 
postseason win since dominating South Carolina 
28-0 in the 2018 Belk Bowl.

• Virginia finished 6-1 in games contested at Scott 
Stadium in 2025. The Cavaliers' six home wins 
were their most in the previous 34 years.

• UVA played in four overtime contests in 2025 
and achieved a 3-1 in such games. The Cavaliers 
knocked off then-No. 8 Florida State in double 
overtime, and on the road at both Louisville and 
North Carolina. UVA's lone overtime loss came 
at the hands of Duke in the ACC championship 
game.

• UVA was 16th in the final AP poll, its highest 
ranking since 1995 and first appearance in the 
final poll since 2004. The Cavaliers were also 
ranked in each of the six College Football Playoff 
rankings for the first time ever.

• Behind one of the league's best offensive lines, 
UVA tailback J'Mari Taylor finished as the ACC's 
regular-season leading rusher with 997 yards.

###### PROTECT THE BALL, PROTECT THE QB

• Virginia head coach **Tony Elliott** has said that 
the ball and the quarterback “are the program,” 
underscoring the central role both play in a team’s 
success.

• UVA did not turn the ball over in its 34-8 seasonopening win over NC State (8/29).

• In 2025, UVA tied for 13th nationally in fumbles 
lost (4). The Cavaliers entered Week 12 against 
Wake Forest (Nov. 8) as one of two teams 
nationally to not lose a fumble, but lost three in 
that game.

• UVA finished third in the ACC in turnovers lost per 
game (14).

• The Cavaliers did not allow a sack in five games 
last season, including their first three, which 
marked the first time since 2011 that UVA went 
three consecutive games without giving up a sack.

###### CAVALIERS REFORTIFY ON BYE WEEKS

• Under fifth-year head coach **Tony Elliott,** Virginia 
is 7-0 all-time in games immediately following an 
idle week.

• Two of those wins came on the road against 
ranked opponents, highlighted by a victory over 
then-No. 10 North Carolina — the highest-ranked 
road win in program history.

• UVA defeated Norfolk State (Sept. 11) 59-3 and 
will look to tally its eighth straight win coming off a 
bye when the Cavaliers host Cal (Nov. 14).

###### MORE ON THE 2026 SLATE

• For the second consecutive season, Virginia will 
play seven home games at Scott Stadium in 
2026, including five ACC contests.

• Of UVA’s 12 opponents, six (NC State, Delaware, 
SMU, Duke, Wake Forest and Cal) appeared in a 
bowl game in 2025. All except for Cal won their 
bowl games.

• The Hoos will host two first-time opponents, 
Norfolk State (Sept. 11) and Delaware (Sept. 26).

• In 2025, UVA posted a 5-3 record against teams 
it will face again in 2026 with wins over Cal, Duke 
North Carolina, Florida State and Virginia Tech.

• The Cavaliers host Duke and North Carolina at 
Scott Stadium in the same season for the first 
time since 2020, when UVA won both contests.

###### SUPER, (SUPER, SUPER) SENIORS

• Virginia leads the nation with 31 combined players 
in at least their fifth year of college (16 5th-years, 
six sixth-years, eight seventh-years and one eighthyear).

• UVA's s lone eighth-year is returner **Ja'Maric 
Morris** (DB), while its seventh-years include 
returners **Drake Metcalf** (OL), **Monroe Mills** (OL) 
and **Daniel Sparks** (P), and transfers **Nnanna 
Anyanwu** (DE), **Kervins Choute** (DL), **Darrion 
Henry-Young** (DT) and **Lukas Ungar** (TE).

• The Cavaliers' group of sixth-year players includes 
**Tyson Davis** (WR), **Noah Josey** (OL), **Jalen McNair**
(S), **Makilan Thomas** (OL) and **Justin Townsend**
(DL).

• Of UVA's sixth-, seventh- and eighth-year players, 
Josey is the only Cavalier who has played at UVA 
for the entirety of his college career. This year 
marks Sparks' fifth season in Charlottesville.

• In 2025, Virginia was the only FBS program with 
19 combined sixth- and seventh-year players.

• Additionally, UVA and Colorado were the only two 
FBS schools with three seventh-year players on 
their 2025 rosters.

###### BULKING SZN

• Virginia's strength program is led by head football 
strength coach **Adam Smotherman.**

• This past offseason, the Cavaliers lost 274 
pounds of fat and 713 pounds of muscle, a 41% 
improvement from the 2025 offseason.

###### COMMITTMENT TO ACADEMICS

• At his introductory press conference head coach 
**Tony Elliott** stated, “I chose UVA because I 
wanted to lead a program that was partnered 
with a university that had world-class academics. 
I wanted to be able to recruit the best and the 
brightest student-athletes ... I wanted to be part 
of a university and athletic department that would 
not compromise its values to win.”

• “My vision for the UVA football program is to 
become the model in college football, the model 
program in college football,” Elliott said. “My 
goal is to contribute to changing the narrative in 
college football and demonstrate that you can 
win at the highest level and you can do so while 
achieving excellence in education, leadership and 
service.”

• In 2002, Elliott earned his bachelor’s degree in 
industrial engineering from Clemson, where he 
was a first-team All-ACC Academic selection and a 
CoSIDA Academic District III member. He was also 
a recipient of the ACC’s Weaver-James-Corrigan 
Postgraduate Scholarship.

###### CARLA WILLIAMS ON CFP COMMITTEE

• On Feb. 8, 2024, Virginia Director of Athletics 
**Carla Williams** was named to the College Football 
Playoff (CFP) Management Committee.

• As a member of the committee, Williams began a 
three-year term beginning in the spring of 2024.

• “Carla is an outstanding advocate for college 
football and will be a knowledgeable, passionate 
and valuable member of the College Football 
Playoff Selection Committee,” said ACC 
Commissioner Jim Phillips. “Throughout her time 
at Virginia as well as her entire career, Carla has 
consistently shown to be a tremendous leader.”

•  Williams was appointed Director of Athletics at 
UVA in October 2017, making history as the first 
female Director of Athletics at UVA and the first 
African American female athletics director at a 
Power Five conference institution.

• During her time in Charlottesville, UVA teams 
have captured 12 national titles across numerous 
sports, including the 2019 men’s basketball 
title, 2019 and 2021 men’s lacrosse titles, 
six consecutive women’s swimming and diving 
championships from 2021-26, and men’s tennis 
titles in 2022, 2023 and 2026.

• Additionally, UVA entered the 2026-27 academic 
year having won won 26 total ACC Championships 
during Williams’ tenure at Virginia.

• Williams was a three-year starter and All-SEC 
guard for the Georgia women’s basketball team 
from 1987-89. Williams then served as an 
assistant coach for the team before moving into 
administration as UGA’s assistant director of 
compliance for a year before taking administrative 
roles at Florida State and Vanderbilt.

###### ACC SCHEDULE FORMAT

• Athlon Sports Second Team Preseason All-ACC

###### #91 JASON HAMMOND, DT

• Phil Steele Fourth Team Preseason All-ACC

• Beginning in 2026, the ACC introduced a 
new schedule structure through a transitional 
season designed to balance competitive equity, 
honor existing non-conference game contracts 
and account for the league’s unique 17-team 
footprint. Twelve teams (including UVA) will play 
a 9+1 schedule (nine ACC games plus at least 
one Power Four non-conference opponent) while 
five teams will play an 8+2 schedule (eight ACC 
games paired with at least two Power Four nonconference matchups).

• Starting in 2027, 16 teams will play a ninegame ACC schedule, with one team playing eight 
league games while scheduling two Power Four 
non-conference opponents to fulfill the league's 
required minimum of 10 P4 games.

THE ROUND AISLE

UVA held its first graduation ceremony – referred to as “Final Exercises “ – in 1829, following Thomas Jefferson’s original 
plan that omitted commencement and diplomas. Since 1953, Final Exercises have been scheduled to take place on The 
Lawn, weather permitting.

###### LEGACY PATCHES

To honor Lavel Davis Jr., Devin Chandler and 
D’Sean Perry the No. 1 (worn by Montino Williams 
and Rico Flores Jr.), No. 15 (worn by Dillon 
Newton-Short and Lukas Sanker) and No. 41 
(worn by Will Bettridge) jerseys will each feature 
a legacy patch. Bettridge, who wore No. 17 as a 
first-year, switched to No. 41 in 2023 to honor his 
former high school teammate, Perry. Each year 
the staff will seek input from the families of Davis, 
Chandler and Perry about who will wear Nos. 1, 15 
and 41.

###### HEAD COACH TONY ELLIOTT

###### UVA TENURE & MILESTONES

• **Current Role:** In his fifth season (2026) as the 41st head football coach in 
Virginia history.

• **Historic 2025 Campaign:** Directed the winningest season in UVA history, 
culminating in a TaxSlayer Gator Bowl victory, a top finish in the ACC regularseason standings, and a No. 16 final AP ranking (the program's highest since 
1995).

• **Hardware:** Swept 2025 coaching honors, including ACC Coach of the Year (AP 
and peers), Bear Bryant ACC COY, and the Touchdown Club of Richmond's Bobby 
Ross COY. Finalist for three National Coach of the Year awards.

###### CULTURE & ACADEMICS

• **Leadership Through Adversity:** Guided the University and football program 
through the tragic November 2022 campus shooting. Rebuilt the team’s 
foundation on compassion, family, and his core H.E.A.R.T. principles (Humility, 
Effort, Accountability, Respect, Toughness).

• **"The Model Program":** Committed to winning without compromising values or 
academics. The team posted a program-record 3.201 GPA in Fall 2025, yielding 
14 ACC All-Academic selections and a Jim Tatum Award winner (Will Bettridge).

###### PLAYER DEVELOPMENT

• **NFL Pipeline:** Has produced notable pro talent at UVA, including record-setting 
WR Malik Washington (Dolphins), WR Dontayvion Wicks (Packers/Eagles), and DB 
Jonas Sanker (Saints, UVA's highest defensive draft pick since 2019).

• **ACC Standouts:** Coached 14 All-ACC honorees in 2025, including regular-season 
rushing champion J’Mari Taylor.

###### PRE-UVA & CLEMSON PEDIGREE

•

**Championship Resume:** Spent 11 seasons as an assistant/coordinator 
at Clemson under Dabo Swinney, helping the Tigers capture two national 
championships (2016, 2018) and six ACC titles.

•

**Elite Offensive Mind:** Won the 2017 Frank Broyles Award as the nation’s top 
assistant coach. Mentored generational talents like Trevor Lawrence and NCAA 
record-holder Travis Etienne.

•

**Background:** Former walk-on wide receiver at Clemson who worked his way up 
to team co-captain. Earned a degree in industrial engineering and worked at 
Michelin before entering coaching. Married to Tamika with two sons, A.J. and 
Ace.

EverBank EverBank

# #7 Beau Pribula, QB

6-2 • 209 • Gr. • York, Pa. • Central York • Missouri/Penn State

###### BIG-STAGE BEAU

• Signal caller **Beau Pribula** transferred to Virginia in January 2026 after stops at Missouri 
(2025) and Penn State (2022-24), bringing a wealth of high-level college football experience to 
Charlottesville.

• After winning the starting job for the Tigers during fall camp, he started in all 10 games he 
appeared in last season .

• Pribula finished third in the SEC in completion percentage (67.4%) and passed for 1,941 yards 
and 11 TDs, while rushing for six more scores.

• He earned Maxwell Award National Player of the Week, Davey O’Brien “Great Eight”, and PFF 
National Team of the Week honors following a 334-yard, three-touchdown performance against 
Kansas (Sept. 6, 2025).

###### HAPPY VALLEY

• In three seasons at Penn State, Pribula appeared in 24 games from 2023-24 while backing 
up Drew Allar and carving out a role as one of the nation’s most effective change-of-pace 
quarterbacks.

• Pribula helped the Nittany Lions to multiple New Year’s Six bowl appearances and the College 
Football Playoff (CFP) before transferring to Missouri for the 2025 season.

• He also accounted for 19 touchdowns as a Nittany Lion, including 10 rushing TDs.

###### PENNSYLVANIA PRODUCT

• A native of York, Pennsylvania, Pribula starred at Central York High School, where he became one of 
the state’s top quarterback prospects.

• He finished his prep career with more than 6,000 yards of total offense and earned all-state 
recognition while leading Central York to multiple playoff appearances.

• Pribula was rated among Pennsylvania’s top recruits before signing with Penn State.

###### YOU DON’T KNOW BEAU

• Known for his athleticism, Pribula has proven capable of making plays through the air and with his 
legs.

• He was frequently utilized in short-yardage and red-zone situations at Penn State, developing a 
reputation for extending drives and finishing possessions.

• His versatility gives Virginia an experienced quarterback capable of stressing defenses in multiple 
ways.

###### FOOTBALL FAMILY

• Beau’s father, Tad, was a four-year letterwinner at wide receiver at Shippensburg. Tad later went into 
college administration serving as the Director of Athletics at Shippensburg from 1982-2001 before 
being inducted into the Shippensburg Athletic Hall of Fame (2002), Chester County Sports of Hall 
of Fame (2013) and West Chester Halls of Fame.

• His older brother, Cade, also played quarterback collegiately at Delaware and Sacred Heart, while 
his younger brother, Ty, is a quarterback at Penn State.

• His grandfather, Jim Pribula, was an AP All-American at West Chester before serving as 
Shippensburg’s athletic director from 1982-2001.

• His aunt, Shari (Pribula) Schalge lettered two years in field hockey at Penn State (1984-85).

*“Expectations are high.* **_That’s just the way we want it._** 
*That’s the way the standard should be. Every time we take 
the field, the expectation should be to win.”*

— **_Beau Pribula, QB_**

# #5 Kam Robinson, LB

###### 6-2 • 239 • Sr. • Tappahannock, Va. • Essex High School

###### PROGRAM CHANGER

• Head coach Tony Elliott labeled Robinson a "program-changer" and a "diamond in the rough" for Virginia's 
recruiting efforts.

• He was a coveted four-star prospect. Robinson was also ranked as the No. 8 overall recruit in the 
Commonwealth of Virginia.

• He honored his commitment to the Cavaliers over late recruiting pushes and official visits to traditional powers 
like Florida State and South Carolina, while also holding offers from Penn State and Tennessee.

• During the recruiting process, Virginia's staff made him such a priority that eight UVA coaches attended one of 
his high school basketball games.

###### ESSEX EXCELLENCE

• A multi-sport star at Essex High School in Tappahannock, Virginia, Robinson excelled in football, basketball, and 
baseball.

• He rarely left the football field during his high school career. Robinson featured as a linebacker, wide receiver, 
kick returner, and even punted.

ACC 5 5 SAGAPOLUTEL

###### CLUTCH CAVALIER

• Robinson earned Third-Team All-ACC honors in 2025 following a spectacular junior campaign.

• He developed a reputation for game-altering plays in 2025. Robinson recorded a game-winning safety in a 22-
20 victory against Washington State.

• Two weeks later, he sealed a 31-21 road win at Cal with a 35-yard interception return for a touchdown with just 
34 seconds remaining.

• In 2023 as a true freshman, Robinson was an immediate force. He racked up 71 total tackles, 4.5 tackles for 
loss, two interceptions, and a touchdown.

## UNC DO-IT-ALL DEFENDER

###### DO-IT-ALL DEFENDER

• Before suffering a season-ending torn ACL in Week 12 against Duke last season, Robinson compiled 64 tackles 
and two sacks in just eight games.

• He stuffed the 2025 stat sheet in all phases of the game. Robinson recorded two interceptions, two touchdowns, 
a fumble recovery, a pass deflection, and a blocked punt.

# Game 3 • West Virginia 38, Virginia 27 Saturday, Sept. 19 • Charlotte, N.C./Bank of America Stadium

###### Virginia Team Notes

• UVA’s three-game win streak against non-conference opponents came to a close.

• The Cavaliers dropped to 1-3 against the Big 12 conference all-time.

• Virginia dipped to 3-2 all-time at Bank of America Stadium, home of the NFL’s Carolina Panthers.

• The Cavaliers’ seven-game win streak in games contested in the months of August and September came to a close. UVA had won its previous 
five games in the month of September.

• West Virginia totaled 437 yards of total offense, the most by a UVA opponent since FSU recorded 514 in UVA’s 47-38 double-overtime 
triumph on Sept. 26, 2025. UVA had held its last opponent to 260 yards or less in its last three games, dating back to the 2025 finale 
against No. 25 Missouri in the TaxSlayer Gator Bowl.

• Virginia fell to 12-11-1 all-time against West Virginia and Saturday was the first regular-season meeting between the two schools since 
1985. The Mountaineer victory also snapped the Cavaliers’ three-game win streak in the series, dating back to 1984.

• UVA rushed for 157 yards. It had rushed for more than 200 yards in its first two games of the season against NC State (8/29) and Norfolk 
State (9/11).

• Neither UVA nor WVU committed a turnover. The Cavaliers have now forced at least one turnover in 12 of their last 14 games. In that span, 
UVA has forced a total of 23 turnovers (17 INT, 6 FR).

• Ten Cavaliers recorded a reception in the contest. It marked the second-straight game UVA had 10 or more pass catchers.

• UVA entered Saturday’s having not allowed more than two touchdowns from scrimmage in regulation in its previous seven games.

• For the second consecutive game, UVA scored a touchdown on its opening possession, a 12-play, 62-yard drive that took 6:58. UVA had 
three drives in the contest of 10 plays or more: two resulted in touchdowns – the other a missed field goal.

• Virginia won the time of possession battle, holding the ball for 33:07. The loss snapped a nine-game winning streak when possessing the 
ball for 30 minutes or more.

• For the first time this season, UVA trailed after WVU scored its third touchdown of the game to grasp a 21-14 lead with 26 seconds 
remaining in the first half.

• The last time UVA trailed at halftime was against No. 25 Missouri in the 2025 TaxSlayer Gator Bowl and the last time it trailed by at least 
seven points was against Washington State (10/18/25), when it fell behind 17-7.

• UVA entered the fourth quarter trailing 28-21. It marked the first time UVA trailed after three quarters since the 2025 Wazzu game, when the 
Cavaliers overcame a 20-10 fourth-quarter deficit and prevailed 22-20.

###### Virginia Individual Player Notes

• Running back Jekail Middlebrook rushed 17 times for 75 yards and scored his first touchdown as a Cavalier on an 8-yard rush to open the 
scoring for UVA. It was his ninth collegiate rushing touchdown.

• Middlebrook added a 1-yard touchdown reception in the third quarter, the fifth touchdown catch of his career. It marked the second time of 
his career he recorded a rushing and receiving touchdown in the same game (10/29/25 vs. Jacksonville State while at MTSU).

• Middlebrook is the second Cavalier this season to record a rushing and receiving touchdown in the same game, joining fellow running back 
Xavier Brown, who did so in UVA’s season opener against NC State. It’s the first time since 2021 that UVA has had two different players 
accomplish the feat in the same season (2021 - Keytaon Thompson at Pitt & Brennan Armstrong at BYU).

• Xavier Brown went over the 1,000-yard rushing mark for his career after totaling 71 yards on eight carries. He is one of two Cavalier running 
backs (Middlebrook) on the 2026 roster with 1,000 career rushing yards.

• Brown’s 47-yard touchdown run at the end of the first quarter was his second rushing touchdown of the season (third TD overall) and UVA's 
longest play of the game.

• Defensive end Fisher Camac led the Cavaliers with a career-high 11 tackles (3 solo). The 11 tackles are the most by a UVA defensive 
lineman since Eli Harold had 11 against BYU in 2013.

• Receiver Kameron Courtney led the Cavaliers with five receptions, one shy of matching his career high.

• Receiver Rico Flores Jr.’s first reception as Cavalier came on a crucial fourth-down conversion in the third quarter that went for 26 yards and 
ultimately led to a game-tying touchdown (21-21).

• Running back Xay Davis finished with career highs in both receptions (3) and receiving yards (51). His 35-yard reception in the fourth 
quarter was the longest of his career and led to UVA’s fourth touchdown on its final drive of the game.

• Punter Daniel Sparks established a career best with four punts inside the 20. He also went over 11,000 yards punting in his career in the 
contest.

• Offensive lineman McKale Boley made his 25th consecutive start.

• UVA second-year safety Montino Williams and graduate defensive tackle Darrion Henry-Young made their first career start.

• Defensive end Matthew Fobbs-White tallied a career high of five total tackles.

• For the second straight game, Charlotte native and cornerback Jacobie Henderson finished with a career high in total tackles. He tallied 10 
tackles, including three solo. Henderson had a then-career-best eight tackles in UVA’s previous game against Norfolk State (9/11).

• Linebacker Kam Robinson tied his career high with two tackles for-loss.

• In his second career start, tight end John Rogers scored his second career touchdown on a 2-yard pass from Beau Pribula, which capped 
UVA’s final drive of the night. Rogers’ first career start was in the 2025 TaxSlayer Gator Bowl against No. 25 Missouri.

###### Game 2 • Virginia 59, Norfolk State 3 Friday, Sept. 11 • Charlottesville, Va./Scott Stadium

###### Virginia Team Notes

• Virginia’s 59 points are the most in a game since also posting 59 against Western Michigan in 2003.

• UVA's 56-point margin of victory is its largest since 1990 in a 59-0 win over Duke. The Cavaliers have won only three games since 1946 by 
more than 56 points.

• Friday's result marked the second time under head coach Tony Elliott that Virginia has scored at least 50 points in a game, having done so 
against William & Mary in 2025.

• UVA improved to 7-0 under head coach Elliott in games immediately after a bye week.

• UVA is 2-0 for the second time under Elliott (2024 & 2026).

• For the second-straight game, Virginia rushed for more than 200 yards, accumulating 237 on 38 attempts. UVA has rushed for 200 or more 
yards seven times in its last 15 games.

• Virginia forced three turnovers in the contest and improved to 11-1 when forcing a turnover since the beginning of the 2025 season.

• The Virginia defense has forced a turnover in 12 of its last 13 games. In that span, the Cavaliers have forced a total of 23 turnovers (17 INT, 
6 FR).

• Virginia tallied its 10th straight victory over an FCS opponent.

• UVA earned its third straight win over a non-conference opponent and third straight win at home.

• The Cavaliers are also now winners of eight of their last nine home games since the beginning of the 2025 season.

• UVA earned its second-straight victory on a Friday night. The Wahoos triumphed over then-No. 8 Florida State 46-38 in double overtime at 
Scott Stadium last season on Friday, Sept. 26.

###### Virginia Individual Player Notes

• Kicker Will Bettridge became Virginia’s all-time scoring leader after a 10-point night (1-1 FG, 7-7 XP). He surpassed Connor Hughes’ (2002-
05) mark of 332 points and Bettridge bumped his career total to 337. His seven extra points matched a career best.

• Friday was offensive lineman Noah Josey's 50th game of his career and his 45th consecutive start.

• Quarterback Beau Pribula passed for three touchdowns, all in the first quarter. The three TDs matched his career high previously reached 
against Kansas last season while playing for Missouri.

• Prior to a batted ball that led to an interception in the second quarter, Pribula completed 19 of his first 22 passes of his UVA career (9-11 
vs. NC State & 10 of his first 11 passes vs. Norfolk State). Through his first two games of the season, Pribula has completed 84 percent of 
his passes (21-25) and thrown for five touchdowns.

• Safety Brandyn Hillman intercepted passes on Norfolk State’s first two drives, both of which led to UVA touchdowns. The two-interception 
performance was the first by a Cavalier since Ja’son Prevard against Florida State in 2025. Hillman was responsible for UVA’s first four 
turnovers of the season (1 FF, 1 INT vs. NC State & 2 INT vs. Norfolk State).

• Linebacker Justin Rowe recorded his first career interception and took it 50 yards for a touchdown, the first pick-six since Kam Robinson at 
Cal in 2025.

• Both Rowe and Hillman recorded 50-yard interception returns, the longest by a Cavalier since Fentrell Cypress’ 66-yarder against North 
Carolina in 2021.

• Tailback Xay Davis led the Cavaliers in rushing with 107 yards on 11 carries, eclipsing the 100-yard mark for the first time in his career. He is 
one of five UVA running backs with a 100-yard rushing effort in their collegiate career.

• Linebacker Kam Robinson, who tore his ACL in UVA's 11th game of the 2025 season at Duke (Nov. 15), reached the 200-tackle mark for his 
career with his first tackle of the game in the first quarter. He finished the game with five (two solo) and now has 204 tackles for his career.

• Defensive end Fisher Camac, who finished with three tackles, including 0.5 sacks, eclipsed the 100-career tackle mark. Camac now has 102 
career tackles.

• Wide receiver Jacquon Gibson scored his first touchdown as a Cavalier and second of his collegiate career. He caught a 6-yard pass from 
Beau Pribula to open the UVA scoring. It was his first touchdown since the 2024 season while at UMass.

• Gibson established a career-high with his second touchdown reception late in the first quarter. He’s the first Cavalier with multiple touchdown 
receptions in a game since Trell Harris had three against Stanford in 2025.

• Da’Shawn Martin’s caught his second touchdown as a Cavalier and sixth of his collegiate career to make the score 14-0. His first two 
catches as a UVA wideout were touchdowns (47-yarder vs. NC State & 27-yarder vs. Norfolk State).

• Peyton Lewis scored his first touchdown as a Cavalier, late in the second quarter. It was his 11th of his collegiate career. 
• Ty’Lyric Coleman and Isaiah Robinson caught their first career touchdown receptions in the third quarter on passes from Eli Holstein.

• Holstein recorded his first touchdown as a Cavalier on a 1-yard completion to Coleman, which capped a nine-play, 59-yard drive.

• Cole Geer threw his first career touchdown pass to Hayden Rollison on the first play of the fourth quarter. It was also Rollison’s first career 
reception.

• With a 61-yard punt in the third quarter, which was downed at the NSU 1-yard line, Daniel Sparks notched his 17th career punt of at least 
60 yards. His 17 punts of 60 yards or more are the most by any player in UVA history.

• Fourth-year Chris Messmore scored his first collegiate point, converting an extra point on UVA’s final touchdown. The walk-on kicker spent the 
first three years at UVA as an athletic communications intern working various sporting events including home and away football games.

###### Game 1 • Virginia 34, NC State 8 Saturday, Aug. 29 • Charlottesville, Va./Scott Stadium

###### Virginia Team Notes

• The official attendance for Saturday’s season opener was 62,779, the eighth-largest crowd in UVA history and largest 
since the 2008 season opener against USC.

• The victory was No. 700 for the program. UVA became the 39th FBS team to reach 700 all-time wins.

• The Cavaliers snapped a four-game skid in the series with NC State. The win marked the Cavaliers’ first victory over the 
Wolfpack since 2012 and first at Scott Stadium since 2006.

• UVA is 4-1 in season openers under head coach Tony Elliott and 3-0 inthe month of August.

• The Cavaliers finished with 258 total rushing yards, which marked the fifth game of at least 250 rushing yards under 
Elliott, including second time against NC State (2024).

• Virginia’s 23-play, 75-yard drive over the span of 12:15 in the fourth quarter set a pair of school records: the longest 
scoring drive by number of plays and longest scoring drive by time elapsed. The drive included four fourth-down 
conversions.

• UVA was 9-for-11 passing (81.8%), which marks its best completion percentage since 2018 (vs. Ohio–25-30) and best 
against a P5 opponent since 2004 (vs. North Carolina–13-16).

• Virginia’s 11 pass attempts are the fewest in single-game history, dating back to 1995 (via Sports-Reference.com).

• The Cavalier defense held NC State to just 168 yards of total offense (76 rush, 89 pass). The168 yardsare the fewest 
by a Cavalier opponent since limiting VMI to 78 yards in 2013. It’s also the fewest yards allowed versus a P4 opponent 
since 2006, when Duke had 100 (-21 rushing and 121 passing).

• NC State’s offense averaged four plays and 16 yards per drive.

• The 89 yards passing by NC State Quarterback C.J. Bailey was his lowest output in 23 collegiate starts and the first time 
he was held under100 yardspassing.

• NC State did not score on its first eight possessions, including four three-and-outs.

• The Wolfpack's touchdown in the third quarter was the first against a UVA defense since the first drive of the Gator Bowl, 
a total of 96:57.

• Dating back to 2025, Virginia improved to 10-1whenforcing a turnover.

###### Virginia Individual Player Notes

• In his UVA debut, quarterback Beau Pribula rushed for a career-high110 yards, including a long of 22. He averaged 7.3 
yards per carry. Hisprevious best was 78 yards at Arkansas last year, also his final game as a Missouri Tiger.

• The last UVA quarterback to rush for100 yardswas Brennan Armstrong in UVA’s 2022 season opener against Richmond, 
when Armstrong had 105.

• Pribula (9-11/.818) is the first UVA starting QB since Bryce Perkins (21-26/.808) to complete at least 80% of his 
passes against a P5 opponent since 2018 (vs. Ga. Tech)

• Pribula is also the first UVA starting QB to record at least an 81.8 completion percentage against a P5 opponent since 
Marques Hagans (10-12/.833) did so on Sept. 11,2004,against North Carolina, also his first career start at Scott 
Stadium.

• Xavier Brown recorded his fourth career rushing touchdown and first since a 4-yard score against William & Mary last 
season. Three of his four career rushing scores have come against ACC foes.

• The last two occasions a UVA player has rushed a touchdown and caught a touchdown in the same game have been by 
Xavier Brown (NC State–2026 & Pitt–2024).

• D’Shawn Martin tallied his fifth career touchdown on a 47-yard reception from Beau Pribula on UVA's first drive of the 
second half.

• Xay Davis recorded his first career touchdown. It capped UVA’s 23-play, 75-yard drive in the fourth quarter.

• Brandyn Hillman intercepted the second pass of his career on a tipped ball at the line by Fisher Camac in the first 
quarter.

• Hillman forced a fumble on the UVA 7-yard line that was recovered by Landon Danley, his first fumble recovery of his 
career.

• Myles Brown finished with a team-high eight total tackles, all of which wereassisted.

• Will Bettridge opened the scoring by matching a career long 47-yard field goal. He has 327 career points and is now five 
points behind UVA’s all-time leader, Connor Hughes (2002-05)

• Daniel Sparks final punt of the game traveled 63 yards, his 16thcareer punt of 60 or more yards, the most in UVA 
history.
